################################################################################
# AUDIT TARGET 1 — JOB COST VARIANCE ALERT SYSTEM
################################################################################

STATUS: PARTIALLY WIRED

SUMMARY OF EVIDENCE (details below):
- A full alert-rule / sampler / dispatcher system EXISTS in
  server/utils/jobMetricAlerts.ts (3,127 lines) and is event-driven
  (event "execution:missed" + node-cron). Tables: job_metric_alert_rules,
  job_metric_alert_events, job_metric_samples.
- Profitability comparison (estimate vs actual) IS computed in
  server/ai/profitabilityEngine.ts and surfaced in server/portfolioRoutes.ts
  (variance, variancePct, schedule_variance counter), and a
  ProfitabilityAlertRoutes endpoint EXISTS at
  server/profitabilityAlertRoutes.ts.
- Material-budget overrun alert IS wired end-to-end (variance >=
  alertThresholdPercent → notification row created) in
  server/materialTrackingRoutes.ts and server/locationRoutes.ts.
- AI-generated insights into ai_insights ARE inserted by
  server/ai/moduleAnalyzers.ts::saveAnalysisResults() — but that path
  is only triggered when a user runs an AI module analysis (manual /
  on-demand), not on a recurring schedule. No cron/event hook found
  that runs cost-variance analysis automatically per project.
- KPI snapshot population (project_kpi_snapshots) goes through
  server/services/portfolioKpiSnapshotService.ts; the table is created
  by server/utils/ensureProjectKpiSnapshotsTable.ts. No cron schedule
  for it was located in repo grep (no cron.schedule entry calls into
  the KPI snapshot service — see GAPS).

----- FILE: server/utils/jobMetricAlerts.ts (3127 lines — top 220 lines + dispatch function) -----
/**
 * Task #718 — Alert engine for the in-process job metrics surface.
 *
 * Reads the rolling 24h window from `jobMetrics.ts`, evaluates two
 * kinds of conditions per job/counter, and emits a notification to
 * every Super Admin when a threshold is crossed:
 *
 *   - `spike`  : last-hour total of a counter exceeds
 *                `threshold * baseline_avg_per_hour` AND meets an
 *                absolute minimum (so "1 vs 0.05 baseline" doesn't
 *                trigger noise).
 *   - `stall`  : a job that has previously published samples has gone
 *                silent for at least `threshold` hours.
 *
 * Rules live in `jobMetricAlertRules` and override the built-in
 * defaults (which apply to every job / every counter when there is no
 * explicit row). Fired alerts are recorded in `jobMetricAlertEvents`
 * so the dashboard can show current alert state and the engine can
 * dedupe — within a rule's `dedupeMinutes` window the same
 * job+counter+kind will not fire twice.
 */

import { db } from "../db";
import { and, desc, eq, gt, gte, lte, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
import { alias } from "drizzle-orm/pg-core";
import {
  jobMetricAlertEvents,
  jobMetricAlertQuietHoursDigestSends,
  jobMetricAlertRules,
  users,
  type JobMetricAlertRule,
} from "@shared/schema";

import { getAllJobMetrics, type JobCounters } from "./jobMetrics";
import { getCronMissedTicksSnapshot } from "./cronMissedTicks";
import { logError, logInfo } from "./logger";
import { insertNotifications } from "../services/notificationService";
import { sendEmail } from "../services/emailService";

function buildDisplayName(
  firstName: string | null | undefined,
  lastName: string | null | undefined,
  email: string | null | undefined,
): string | null {
  const full = `${firstName ?? ""} ${lastName ?? ""}`.trim();
  if (full) return full;
  return email ?? null;
}

const HOUR_MS = 60 * 60 * 1000;

export const DEFAULT_SPIKE_MULTIPLIER = 3;
export const DEFAULT_SPIKE_MIN_ABSOLUTE = 5;
export const DEFAULT_STALL_HOURS = 4;
export const DEFAULT_DEDUPE_MINUTES = 60;
// Built-in email-escalation thresholds — applied when a rule does not
// specify its own `emailWhenAbove`. Tuned to "high-severity only" so
// Super Admins are not flooded with routine spikes.
export const DEFAULT_EMAIL_SPIKE_MULTIPLIER = 10;
export const DEFAULT_EMAIL_STALL_HOURS = 12;

// Task #1959 — Special-case absolute-floor defaults for the `skipped`
// counter that overlap guards in `server/jobs/jobRunner.ts` publish
// when a fresh tick is suppressed because the previous tick was still
// in flight. A multiplicative spike rule would never fire on a
// previously-healthy job because the baseline is zero and the engine
// guards against divide-by-zero — so without an absolute floor here,
// chronic skipping would be silent on the alert pipeline even though
// the dashboard shows the trend.
//
// Operators can override per-job/per-counter via the dashboard rules
// table (jobMetricAlertRules) or per-job via `PER_JOB_DEFAULTS` below;
// the env vars give a single global knob without redeploying for the
// common case of "make us less / more aggressive everywhere".
export const SKIPPED_COUNTER = "skipped";
export const DEFAULT_SKIPPED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR)
  : 5;
export const DEFAULT_SKIPPED_DEDUPE_MINUTES = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_DEDUPE_MINUTES),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_DEDUPE_MINUTES)
  : 60;
export const DEFAULT_SKIPPED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_SKIPPED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR)
  : 10;

// Task #2771 — Missed-tick alert rule.
// `cron_missed_ticks_total{job}` is a cumulative counter incremented by
// `scheduleCron()` on each `execution:missed` event (node-cron fires this
// when the event loop was too saturated to honour the scheduled fire time).
// The alert engine tracks per-evaluation deltas so that ">3 missed ticks in
// the last ~15-minute evaluation window" triggers an alert, not the all-time
// total since process start. Operators can override all three env vars without
// redeploying; the per-job `jobMetricAlertRules` table rows also win over the
// built-in defaults as usual.
export const MISSED_TICK_COUNTER = "missedTick";
export const DEFAULT_MISSED_TICK_FLOOR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_FLOOR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_FLOOR)
  : 3;
export const DEFAULT_MISSED_TICK_DEDUPE_MINUTES = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_DEDUPE_MINUTES),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_DEDUPE_MINUTES)
  : 15;
export const DEFAULT_MISSED_TICK_EMAIL_FLOOR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_EMAIL_FLOOR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.JOB_METRIC_MISSED_TICK_EMAIL_FLOOR)
  : 5;

/**
 * Process-local snapshot of the missed-tick counters as of the most
 * recent `evaluateJobMetricAlerts` run. Used to compute per-evaluation
 * deltas: delta = currentSnapshot[job] − lastMissedTickSnapshot[job].
 * Reset to zero on each server restart (matches the source counter).
 */
const _lastMissedTickSnapshot = new Map<string, number>();

/** Test seam — reset the missed-tick snapshot between test cases. */
export function resetMissedTickSnapshotForTests(): void {
  _lastMissedTickSnapshot.clear();
}

// Task #1957 — Configurable thresholds for the synthetic `pool_health`
// job's absolute-floor spike rule on the `exhausted` counter. Each
// `pool_exhausted` event recorded by `recordPoolExhaustedEvent`
// publishes `{ exhausted: 1 }` so the existing alert engine can fire
// when the per-hour rate exceeds these floors.
//
// Defaults are tuned to "page the moment we see the first starvation
// event" (in-app) and "escalate to email once we see a small cluster"
// — connection-pool exhaustion is a hair-on-fire condition that
// directly translates to user-visible request hangs, so we lean
// aggressive. Operators can dial these up via env vars without
// redeploying or override entirely from the dashboard rules table.
export const POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_FLOOR_PER_HOUR)
  : 1;
export const POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR = Number.isFinite(
  Number(process.env.POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR),
)
  ? Number(process.env.POOL_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED_EMAIL_FLOOR_PER_HOUR)
  : 3;

export type AlertKind = "spike" | "stall";

export interface JobMetricAlert {
  job: string;
  counter: string | null;
  kind: AlertKind;
  ruleId: string | null;
  message: string;
  observedValue: number | null;
  baselineValue: number | null;
  emailed: boolean;
}

interface AlertRuleResolved {
  id: string | null;
  job: string;
  counter: string | null;
  kind: AlertKind;
  threshold: number;
  minAbsolute: number;
  dedupeMinutes: number;
  enabled: boolean;
  isDefault: boolean;
  /**
   * Optional severity escalation. When set and the observed value
   * (spike: lastHour count, stall: idle hours) meets or exceeds it,
   * Super Admins receive a SendGrid email in addition to the in-app
   * notification. `null` means "in-app only — no email escalation".
   */
  emailWhenAbove: number | null;
}

let lastEvaluationAt: number | null = null;

/** Test seam — overridable so unit tests can supply their own clock. */
let nowFn: () => number = () => Date.now();
export function setJobMetricAlertsClockForTests(fn: () => number): void {
  nowFn = fn;
}
export function resetJobMetricAlertsClockForTests(): void {
  nowFn = () => Date.now();
}

/**
 * Task #1573 — Side-channel for the retention-policy sweep to publish
 * the offending `(entityType, action)` pairs from each run, so the
 * `policiesUnsupported` spike alert can include them in the message
 * (and SendGrid escalation) without operators having to crack open the
 * warning logs to identify the offenders.
 *
 * The `JobCounters` payload is numeric-only (see `jobMetrics.ts`), so
 * structured per-policy details cannot ride along on the metrics
 * sample. Instead the sweep calls
 * `setRetentionPolicyUnsupportedPairsForAlerts` at the end of each run
 * with the unsupported pairs and the alert engine reads them via the
 * matching getter when constructing the alert message.
 */
export interface UnsupportedRetentionPolicyPair {
  entityType: string;
  action: string;
}

let lastRetentionPolicyUnsupportedPairs: UnsupportedRetentionPolicyPair[] = [];

export function setRetentionPolicyUnsupportedPairsForAlerts(
  pairs: ReadonlyArray<UnsupportedRetentionPolicyPair>,
): void {

271-/**
272- * Task #1417 / #1453 — Per-job override for the built-in defaults. Used
273- * when a job's natural cadence makes the engine-wide defaults (designed
274- * for frequently-ticking jobs like notification fan-out) noisy or wrong.
275- *
276- * The engine ships with `DEFAULT_STALL_HOURS = 4` and
277- * `DEFAULT_EMAIL_STALL_HOURS = 12`, which are appropriate for jobs
278- * that tick every few minutes. Any cron-driven job in
279- * `server/jobs/jobRunner.ts` whose natural cadence is longer than 4h
280- * would otherwise page Super Admins on the very next evaluation tick
281- * after a successful run (the 4h window expires long before the next
282: * scheduled run). The map below tunes the stall threshold (and email
283- * escalation floor) to "tolerate exactly one missed run" for each such
284- * job so an alert only fires after the second consecutive missed run.
285- * Dedupe windows are widened to match the cadence so a sustained
286- * outage doesn't re-page on every 15-minute alert tick.
287- *
288- * No DB seeding is required — `resolveRule` consults this map whenever
289- * no operator-authored rule exists for a (job, counter, kind), so a
290- * fresh deploy is alerted out of the box. Operators can still override
291- * any of these via `jobMetricAlertRules` rows from the dashboard.
292- *
293- * Job names mirror the snake_case identifier passed to
294- * `recordJobMetrics` (where the job already publishes samples) or the
295- * runner's task variable (for jobs that will start publishing later).
296- * Adding a defensive entry now is a no-op until the job publishes its
297- * first sample — `getAllJobMetrics()` only surfaces jobs the engine
298- * has actually seen — but it guarantees the right policy is in place
299- * the moment metrics are wired up.
300- */

----- FILE: server/utils/jobMetrics.ts (full) -----
/**
 * Lightweight job metrics surface.
 *
 * Background jobs call `recordJobMetrics(jobName, counters)` at the end of
 * each tick. We keep a rolling 24h window of samples per job so a Super
 * Admin can hit an admin endpoint to spot regressions (e.g. a DST bug
 * spiking `skippedDedupe`, or an empty-contact week causing
 * `skippedNoBirthdays` to dominate) without needing to grep raw logs.
 *
 * Task #705: samples are now ALSO persisted to the `job_metric_samples`
 * table so the window survives server restarts/deploys. The in-process
 * Map remains the read path (sync API, no DB round-trip per request) and
 * is hydrated from the table at startup via `loadJobMetricsFromDb()`.
 * DB writes are fire-and-forget so a transient DB hiccup never breaks a
 * job tick — the in-memory record always succeeds, and the row will be
 * missing on next restart only if the insert genuinely failed.
 */

import { db } from "../db";
import { jobMetricSamples } from "@shared/schema";
import { and, eq, gte, lt } from "drizzle-orm";
import { logError } from "./logger";

export type JobCounters = Record<string, number>;

export interface JobMetricSample {
  timestamp: number;
  counters: JobCounters;
}

const RETENTION_MS = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
const PERSISTED_RETENTION_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
const MAX_SAMPLES_PER_JOB = 2000;

export type MetricsRangeKey = "24h" | "7d" | "30d";
export const METRICS_RANGES: Record<MetricsRangeKey, number> = {
  "24h": 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,
  "7d": 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,
  "30d": 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,
};

export function parseMetricsRange(value: unknown): MetricsRangeKey {
  if (value === "7d" || value === "30d") return value;
  return "24h";
}

const samplesByJob = new Map<string, JobMetricSample[]>();
let persistenceEnabled = true;

function prune(job: string, now: number): JobMetricSample[] {
  const list = samplesByJob.get(job);
  if (!list) return [];
  const cutoff = now - RETENTION_MS;
  let i = 0;
  while (i < list.length && list[i].timestamp < cutoff) i++;
  if (i > 0) list.splice(0, i);
  if (list.length > MAX_SAMPLES_PER_JOB) {
    list.splice(0, list.length - MAX_SAMPLES_PER_JOB);
  }
  return list;
}

async function persistSample(job: string, sample: JobMetricSample): Promise<void> {
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return;
  try {
    await db.insert(jobMetricSamples).values({
      job,
      recordedAt: new Date(sample.timestamp),
      counters: sample.counters,
    });
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to persist job metric sample", { job, error: String(err) });
  }
}

/**
 * Record a sample of counter values for a job tick. The sample is
 * stamped with the current time, kept in the rolling 24h in-memory
 * window, and asynchronously persisted to `job_metric_samples` so the
 * trend survives restarts.
 */
export function recordJobMetrics(job: string, counters: JobCounters): JobMetricSample {
  const now = Date.now();
  const sample: JobMetricSample = {
    timestamp: now,
    counters: { ...counters },
  };
  const list = samplesByJob.get(job) ?? [];
  list.push(sample);
  samplesByJob.set(job, list);
  prune(job, now);
  // Fire-and-forget — DB outages must never break a job tick.
  void persistSample(job, sample);
  return sample;
}

/**
 * Return the rolling 24h samples for a single job along with
 * convenience aggregates (sum / latest) for the counters seen.
 */
export function getJobMetrics(job: string): {
  job: string;
  windowMs: number;
  samples: JobMetricSample[];
  totals: JobCounters;
  latest: JobMetricSample | null;
} {
  const now = Date.now();
  const samples = prune(job, now).slice();
  const totals: JobCounters = {};
  for (const s of samples) {
    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(s.counters)) {
      // Task #1962 — guard against non-numeric values so jobs that store
      // richer per-run JSONB detail (e.g. dot_pay_items_refresh's
      // per-agency results array) don't poison the numeric totals/aggregates
      // shown on the JobMetricsHealth dashboard.
      if (typeof v !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(v)) continue;
      totals[k] = (totals[k] ?? 0) + v;
    }
  }
  return {
    job,
    windowMs: RETENTION_MS,
    samples,
    totals,
    latest: samples.length ? samples[samples.length - 1] : null,
  };
}

/**
 * Snapshot of every job currently tracked. Useful for a generic admin
 * dashboard listing all known job metrics in one call.
 */
export function getAllJobMetrics(): Array<ReturnType<typeof getJobMetrics>> {
  const now = Date.now();
  for (const job of samplesByJob.keys()) prune(job, now);
  return Array.from(samplesByJob.keys()).sort().map(getJobMetrics);
}

function summarize(job: string, windowMs: number, samples: JobMetricSample[]) {
  const totals: JobCounters = {};
  for (const s of samples) {
    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(s.counters)) {
      // Task #1962 — same numeric guard as `getJobMetrics`; see comment there.
      if (typeof v !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(v)) continue;
      totals[k] = (totals[k] ?? 0) + v;
    }
  }
  return {
    job,
    windowMs,
    samples,
    totals,
    latest: samples.length ? samples[samples.length - 1] : null,
  };
}

/**
 * Range-aware variant. For 24h we use the in-memory rolling window
 * (fast, no DB round-trip). For 7d / 30d we query the persisted
 * `job_metric_samples` table directly so admins can spot slow
 * regressions that span days or weeks.
 */
export async function getJobMetricsForRange(job: string, range: MetricsRangeKey) {
  const windowMs = METRICS_RANGES[range];
  if (range === "24h") {
    const mem = getJobMetrics(job);
    return { ...mem, windowMs };
  }
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return summarize(job, windowMs, []);
  const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - windowMs);
  try {
    const rows = await db
      .select({
        recordedAt: jobMetricSamples.recordedAt,
        counters: jobMetricSamples.counters,
      })
      .from(jobMetricSamples)
      .where(and(eq(jobMetricSamples.job, job), gte(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt, cutoff)))
      .orderBy(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt);
    const samples: JobMetricSample[] = rows.map((r) => ({
      timestamp: new Date(r.recordedAt).getTime(),
      counters: (r.counters as JobCounters) ?? {},
    }));
    return summarize(job, windowMs, samples);
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to load job metrics for range", { job, range, error: String(err) });
    return summarize(job, windowMs, []);
  }
}

export async function getAllJobMetricsForRange(range: MetricsRangeKey) {
  const windowMs = METRICS_RANGES[range];
  if (range === "24h") {
    return getAllJobMetrics().map((j) => ({ ...j, windowMs }));
  }
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return [];
  const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - windowMs);
  try {
    const rows = await db
      .select({
        job: jobMetricSamples.job,
        recordedAt: jobMetricSamples.recordedAt,
        counters: jobMetricSamples.counters,
      })
      .from(jobMetricSamples)
      .where(gte(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt, cutoff))
      .orderBy(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt);
    const byJob = new Map<string, JobMetricSample[]>();
    for (const row of rows) {
      const list = byJob.get(row.job) ?? [];
      list.push({
        timestamp: new Date(row.recordedAt).getTime(),
        counters: (row.counters as JobCounters) ?? {},
      });
      byJob.set(row.job, list);
    }
    return Array.from(byJob.keys())
      .sort()
      .map((job) => summarize(job, windowMs, byJob.get(job)!));
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to load all job metrics for range", { range, error: String(err) });
    return [];
  }
}

/**
 * Hydrate the in-memory window from the persisted samples table. Called
 * once at startup so a fresh process immediately has access to the
 * trailing 24h of data instead of starting empty.
 */
export async function loadJobMetricsFromDb(): Promise<number> {
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return 0;
  const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - RETENTION_MS);
  try {
    const rows = await db
      .select({
        job: jobMetricSamples.job,
        recordedAt: jobMetricSamples.recordedAt,
        counters: jobMetricSamples.counters,
      })
      .from(jobMetricSamples)
      .where(gte(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt, cutoff))
      .orderBy(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt);

    samplesByJob.clear();
    for (const row of rows) {
      const list = samplesByJob.get(row.job) ?? [];
      list.push({
        timestamp: new Date(row.recordedAt).getTime(),
        counters: (row.counters as JobCounters) ?? {},
      });
      samplesByJob.set(row.job, list);
    }
    // Cap each job to MAX_SAMPLES_PER_JOB just in case the DB has more.
    const now = Date.now();
    for (const job of samplesByJob.keys()) prune(job, now);
    return rows.length;
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to hydrate job metrics from DB", { error: String(err) });
    return 0;
  }
}

/**
 * Delete persisted samples older than the retention window. Called
 * periodically by the job runner to keep the table bounded.
 */
export async function pruneJobMetricsDb(): Promise<number> {
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return 0;
  const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - PERSISTED_RETENTION_MS);
  try {
    const deleted = await db
      .delete(jobMetricSamples)
      .where(lt(jobMetricSamples.recordedAt, cutoff))
      .returning({ id: jobMetricSamples.id });
    return deleted.length;
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to prune persisted job metrics", { error: String(err) });
    return 0;
  }
}

/**
 * Test helper — drop all in-memory samples (does not touch the DB).
 */
export function resetJobMetrics(): void {
  samplesByJob.clear();
}

/**
 * Clear stored job metric history. Wipes both the in-memory rolling
 * window and the persisted `job_metric_samples` rows. When `job` is
 * provided only that job's samples are removed; otherwise every job is
 * cleared. Returns the number of DB rows deleted.
 *
 * Used by the Super Admin "reset metrics" endpoint so a known-bad
 * deploy or noisy backfill can be flushed without manual SQL.
 */
export async function clearJobMetrics(job?: string): Promise<number> {
  if (job) {
    samplesByJob.delete(job);
  } else {
    samplesByJob.clear();
  }
  if (!persistenceEnabled) return 0;
  try {
    const deleted = job
      ? await db
          .delete(jobMetricSamples)
          .where(eq(jobMetricSamples.job, job))
          .returning({ id: jobMetricSamples.id })
      : await db.delete(jobMetricSamples).returning({ id: jobMetricSamples.id });
    return deleted.length;
  } catch (err) {
    logError("Failed to clear persisted job metrics", { job: job ?? "*", error: String(err) });
    return 0;
  }
}

/**
 * Test helper — disable DB persistence so unit tests don't hit the DB.
 * Kept module-private semantics by default; tests opt-in explicitly.
 */
export function setJobMetricsPersistenceEnabled(enabled: boolean): void {
  persistenceEnabled = enabled;
}

----- FILE: server/utils/jobMetricsConfirmation.ts (full) -----
/**
 * Task #717: confirmation guard for the destructive
 * `DELETE /api/admin/metrics/jobs[/:jobName]` endpoints.
 *
 * Without an explicit confirmation token, a misclick or a stale `curl`
 * sitting in someone's terminal history could permanently wipe persisted
 * job metric trend data with no recovery. We require either a query
 * string or a JSON body field that matches an expected token before
 * allowing the request through. The check is intentionally case- and
 * whitespace-sensitive so it cannot be satisfied accidentally.
 *
 *   DELETE /api/admin/metrics/jobs              ?confirm=all
 *   DELETE /api/admin/metrics/jobs/:jobName     ?confirm=<jobName>
 *
 * Either form may also be supplied as `{ "confirm": "..." }` in the
 * JSON request body so it remains scriptable from places where building
 * a query string is awkward.
 */

import type { Request, Response, NextFunction } from "express";

export const CLEAR_ALL_JOB_METRICS_CONFIRM_TOKEN = "all";

function readConfirmToken(req: Request): string | undefined {
  const fromQuery = req.query?.confirm;
  if (typeof fromQuery === "string" && fromQuery.length > 0) {
    return fromQuery;
  }
  const fromBody = (req.body as { confirm?: unknown } | undefined)?.confirm;
  if (typeof fromBody === "string" && fromBody.length > 0) {
    return fromBody;
  }
  return undefined;
}

export interface JobMetricsConfirmationFailure {
  error: string;
  expected: string;
  hint: string;
}

export function buildConfirmationFailure(expected: string): JobMetricsConfirmationFailure {
  const hint =
    expected === CLEAR_ALL_JOB_METRICS_CONFIRM_TOKEN
      ? "Pass ?confirm=all (or { \"confirm\": \"all\" }) to wipe every persisted job metric sample."
      : `Pass ?confirm=${expected} (or { "confirm": "${expected}" }) to wipe persisted samples for this job.`;
  return {
    error: "Confirmation required to clear job metric history.",
    expected,
    hint,
  };
}

export function requireJobMetricsClearConfirmation(
  expectedFromReq: string | ((req: Request) => string),
) {
  return function confirmationMiddleware(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction): void {
    const expected = typeof expectedFromReq === "function" ? expectedFromReq(req) : expectedFromReq;
    const token = readConfirmToken(req);
    if (token !== expected) {
      res.status(400).json(buildConfirmationFailure(expected));
      return;
    }
    next();
  };
}

----- FILE: server/jobMetricAlertEventsRoutes.ts (full) -----
/**
 * Task #1557 — Extract the alert *event* endpoints (acknowledge / snooze /
 * unsnooze / digests / events.csv / digests/test / run) out of the giant
 * `registerRoutes` function in `server/routes.ts` so they live alongside
 * the rule routes already moved in Task #1540. Behavior is unchanged:
 * same auth, same audit log entries, same response shapes.
 */
import { Router, type Response, type NextFunction } from "express";
import { z } from "zod";
import { requireAuth, requireRole, type AuthRequest } from "./auth";
import { logAudit } from "./auditLogger";
import { parseOrRespond } from "./utils/validation";
import * as jobMetricAlerts from "./utils/jobMetricAlerts";

const router = Router();

// Task #729 — acknowledge / snooze noisy alert events
router.post(
  "/admin/metrics/jobs/alerts/events/:id/acknowledge",
  requireAuth,
  requireRole("SUPER_ADMIN"),
  async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    try {
      const updated = await jobMetricAlerts.acknowledgeJobMetricAlertEvent(
        req.params.id,
        req.user!.id,
      );
      if (!updated) {
        res.status(404).json({ error: "Alert event not found" });
        return;
      }
      await logAudit(
        {
          userId: req.user!.id,
          action: "UPDATE",
          entityType: "job_metric_alert_event",
          entityId: updated.id,
          newValue: `Acknowledged alert ${updated.job}/${updated.counter ?? "_"}/${updated.kind}`,
        },
        req,
      );
      res.json(updated);
    } catch (err: any) {
      next(err);
    }
  },
);

const snoozeSchema = z.object({ hours: z.number().positive().max(7 * 24) });

router.post(
  "/admin/metrics/jobs/alerts/events/:id/snooze",
  requireAuth,
  requireRole("SUPER_ADMIN"),
  async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    try {
      const snoozeParsed = parseOrRespond(snoozeSchema, req.body, res);
      if (!snoozeParsed) return;
      const { hours } = snoozeParsed;
      const updated = await jobMetricAlerts.snoozeJobMetricAlertEvent(
        req.params.id,
        req.user!.id,
        hours,
      );
      if (!updated) {
        res.status(404).json({ error: "Alert event not found" });
        return;
      }
      await logAudit(
        {
          userId: req.user!.id,
          action: "UPDATE",
          entityType: "job_metric_alert_event",
          entityId: updated.id,
          newValue: `Snoozed ${updated.job}/${updated.counter ?? "_"}/${updated.kind} for ${hours}h until ${updated.snoozedUntil ? new Date(updated.snoozedUntil).toISOString() : "n/a"}`,
        },
        req,
      );
      res.json(updated);
    } catch (err: any) {
      if (err instanceof z.ZodError) {
        res
          .status(400)
          .json({ error: "Invalid snooze request", details: err.errors });
        return;
      }
      next(err);
    }
  },
);

// Task #2023 — Surface the active snoozes (keyed by `(job, counter, kind)`)
// so the in-app notification feed can render a persistent
// "Snoozed until X by Y" banner on the matching `job_metric_alert`
// bell row instead of just flashing a toast at snooze time. The
// response also carries the underlying `eventId` so the bell row's
// "Unsnooze" button can hit the existing `:id/unsnooze` endpoint.
router.get(
  "/admin/metrics/jobs/alerts/active-snoozes",
  requireAuth,
  requireRole("SUPER_ADMIN"),
  async (_req: AuthRequest, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    try {
      const snoozes = await jobMetricAlerts.listActiveJobMetricAlertSnoozes();
      res.json({ snoozes });
    } catch (err: any) {
      next(err);
    }
  },
);

// Task #2010 — Snooze the most recent matching `(job, counter, kind)`
// alert event without requiring the caller to know its row id. Powers
// the in-app notification action buttons that page Super Admins from
// `pool_health` starvation events: the bell row only carries the
// dedupe key (e.g. `pool_health:waitingSaturated:spike` in
// `relatedEntityId`), so this endpoint lets the operator act on the
// alert without first navigating to the dashboard. Behavior, dedupe
// key, and audit log entry mirror the `:id/snooze` route exactly.
const snoozeByKeySchema = z.object({
  job: z.string().min(1).max(128),
  counter: z.string().min(1).max(128).nullable().optional(),
  kind: z.enum(["spike", "stall"]),
  hours: z.number().positive().max(7 * 24),
});

router.post(
  "/admin/metrics/jobs/alerts/snooze-by-key",
  requireAuth,
  requireRole("SUPER_ADMIN"),
  async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    try {
      const snoozeKeyParsed = parseOrRespond(snoozeByKeySchema, req.body, res);
      if (!snoozeKeyParsed) return;
      const { job, counter, kind, hours } = snoozeKeyParsed;
      const updated = await jobMetricAlerts.snoozeJobMetricAlertByKey(
        req.user!.id,
        job,
        counter ?? null,
        kind,
        hours,
      );
      if (!updated) {
        res
          .status(404)
          .json({ error: "No matching alert event in the last 24h" });
        return;
      }
      await logAudit(
        {
          userId: req.user!.id,
          action: "UPDATE",
          entityType: "job_metric_alert_event",
          entityId: updated.id,
          newValue: `Snoozed ${updated.job}/${updated.counter ?? "_"}/${updated.kind} for ${hours}h until ${updated.snoozedUntil ? new Date(updated.snoozedUntil).toISOString() : "n/a"} (via notification action)`,
        },
        req,
      );
      res.json(updated);
    } catch (err: any) {
      if (err instanceof z.ZodError) {
        res
          .status(400)
          .json({ error: "Invalid snooze request", details: err.errors });
        return;
      }
      next(err);
    }
  },
);

// Task #734 — clear an active snooze early
router.post(
  "/admin/metrics/jobs/alerts/events/:id/unsnooze",
  requireAuth,
  requireRole("SUPER_ADMIN"),
  async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    try {
      const updated = await jobMetricAlerts.unsnoozeJobMetricAlertEvent(
        req.params.id,
      );
      if (!updated) {
        res.status(404).json({ error: "Alert event not found" });
        return;
      }
      await logAudit(
        {
          userId: req.user!.id,
          action: "UPDATE",
          entityType: "job_metric_alert_event",
          entityId: updated.id,
          newValue: `Cleared snooze on ${updated.job}/${updated.counter ?? "_"}/${updated.kind}`,
        },
        req,
      );
      res.json(updated);
    } catch (err: any) {
      next(err);
    }
  },
);
